. By Corollary 2.1 of
[
2
]
, we can take the Fourier transform and bound this as

\displaystyle I_{121}^{\prime}\geq\frac{1}{16\pi^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\!\left|(H\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}\chi_{A_{j}})^{\wedge}(\zeta)\right|^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\!b_{s}^{(\delta)}\left(\sigma\cdot\frac{\zeta}{\left|\zeta\right|}\right)\left((\underline{M}\chi_{B_{j}})^{\wedge}(0)-(\underline{M}\chi_{B_{j}})^{\wedge}(\zeta^{-})\right)\,{\rm d}\sigma\right)\,{\rm d}\zeta

but by Lemma
A.4
and summing over
1\leq j\leq J
, we can then bound this below as

\displaystyle I_{121}^{\prime}\gtrsim\delta^{2-2s}\left(c_{0}\left\|h\chi_{3R}\right\|_{H^{s}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}^{2}-c_{1}\left\|h\chi_{3R}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}^{2}\right).

We can then treat the term
I_{11}^{\prime}
as in the proof of Theorem 3.1 in
[
35
]
. Observing that
\left|\xi-\xi_{*}\right|\geq R
on the support of the integrand, we can bound

\displaystyle I_{11}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\!b_{s}^{(\delta)}(\cos\theta)(\underline{M}\chi_{R})_{*}(H^{\prime}-H)^{2}(1-\chi_{3R}(\xi))^{2}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma}\,{\rm d}\sigma\,{\rm d}\xi_{*}\,{\rm d}\xi.

We can again immediately use the proof of the truncation lemma in
[
2
]
and the fact that
\gamma\geq 0
to bound this from below by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\!b_{s}^{(\delta)}(\cos\theta)(\underline{M}\chi_{R})_{*}\left(H^{\prime}(1-{\chi_{3R}}^{\prime})\left\langle\xi^{\prime}\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}-H(1-\chi_{3R})\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}\right)^{2}\,{\rm d}\sigma\,{\rm d}\xi_{*}\,{\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\!b_{s}^{(\delta)}(\cos\theta)(\underline{M}\chi_{R})_{*}\left((1-{\chi_{3R}}^{\prime})\left\langle\xi^{\prime}\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}-(1-\chi_{3R})\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}\right)^{2}{H^{\prime}}^{2}\,{\rm d}\sigma\,{\rm d}\xi_{*}\,{\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad=I_{111}^{\prime}-I_{112}^{\prime}.

As with
I_{121}^{\prime}
, we can apply Corollary 2.1 of
[
2
]
and Lemma
A.4
to get the lower bound

\displaystyle I_{111}^{\prime}\gtrsim\delta^{2-2s}\left\|h(1-\chi_{3R})\right\|_{H^{s}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}^{2}-\delta^{2-2s}\left\|h(1-\chi_{3R})\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}^{2}.

Meanwhile, the proof of Theorem 3.1 of
[
35
]
immediately gives the bound

\displaystyle I_{112}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\!b_{s}^{(\delta)}\ (\underline{M}\chi_{R})_{*}\left\langle\xi_{*}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle\xi^{\prime}\right\rangle^{\gamma}\sin(\theta/2)^{2}{H^{\prime}}^{2}\,{\rm d}\sigma\,{\rm d}\xi_{*}\,{\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{2-2s}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}^{2},

for any
\gamma\in[0,2]
, where the first estimate is proved by a mean value argument and the second follows by a pre-post collisional change of variables, and by collecting terms we get the bound

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}I_{1}\gtrsim c_{2}\delta^{2-2s}\left\|h\right\|_{H^{s}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}^{2}-c_{3}\delta^{2-2s}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}^{2}.

Combining this with the bounds (
125
) and (
126
) gives the estimate

\displaystyle\left\langle Q_{s,\gamma}^{(\delta)}(\underline{M},H),H\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\leq-c_{1}\epsilon\left\|h\right\|_{\dot{H}^{s,\gamma,*}_{\xi}(m)}-\left(c_{2}\delta^{2-2s}-c_{3}\right)\left\|h\right\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+c_{4}\delta^{2-2s}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}

for some constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4}>0
. The rest of the proof of Lemma 4.2 of
[
37
]
is then self-contained and holds for
\gamma\in[0,2]
, giving the bounds

\displaystyle\left\langle Q_{s,\gamma}^{(\delta),\mathrm{c}}(\underline{M},h),h\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{\xi}(m)}\lesssim\delta^{-2s}\left(c_{5}\delta^{1/2}-\nu_{0}\right)\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}^{2}+C_{\delta}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}^{2}

and,

\displaystyle R\lesssim c_{6}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}^{2},

while Lemma
A.1
for
k>\gamma/2+2+\max(1/2,2s)
then gives us the bound

\displaystyle\left\langle Q_{s,\gamma}(h,\underline{M}),h\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{\xi}(m)}^{2}\lesssim c_{7}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}^{2}

for some constants
c_{5},\nu_{0},C_{\delta},c_{6},c_{7}>0
.
Gathering these terms, we derive the estimate

\displaystyle\left\langle\underline{L}_{s,\gamma}h,h\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{\xi}(m)}

\displaystyle\leq-\left(c_{2}\delta^{2-2s}-c_{3}\epsilon\right)\left\|h\right\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}-c_{1}\epsilon\left\|h\right\|_{\dot{H}^{s,\gamma,*}_{\xi}(m)}

\displaystyle+\left(c_{6}+\delta^{-2s}\left(c_{4}\delta^{2}+c_{5}\delta^{1/2}-\nu_{0}\right)\right)\left\|h\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\xi}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}+C_{\delta}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}^{2},

and taking
\delta
sufficiently small, and taking small
\varepsilon\lesssim\delta^{2-2s}
proves the lemma.
∎

We now assume (
8
) for the rest of this section, so that
s\in(0,1/2)
and
\gamma\in[0,2]
and
\underline{L}_{\kappa}=\underline{L}_{s,\gamma}+\kappa\underline{L}_{s,2-2s}
, and we decompose
\underline{L}_{\kappa}=\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}
for

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}=M\chi_{R},\qquad\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}=\underline{L}_{\kappa}-M\chi_{R}

where the parameters
M,R>0
may vary. When
\kappa=0
, a coercivity result for
\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}
was proved in Lemma 4.3 of
[
37
]
. We extend this to a uniform coercivity result in
\kappa
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
k\geq 4
and
a<0
, and let
\kappa_{0}>0
. Then there exist
M,R>0
independent of
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{0}]
such that
\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}
satisfies the coercivity estimate

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}f,f\right\rangle_{L^{2}(m)}\leq a\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}^{2}.

Proof.

Using Lemma
A.2
and the definition of
\underline{L}_{\kappa}
, we can estimate

\displaystyle\left\langle\underline{L}_{\kappa}h,h\right\rangle_{L^{2}(m)}

\displaystyle\leq-c_{0}\delta^{2-2s}\left(\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}m)}^{2}+\kappa\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{1-s})}^{2}\right)+C_{\delta}^{\prime}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

and therefore we can express

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}h,h\right\rangle_{L^{2}(m)}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\!\left(-c_{0}\delta^{2-2s}\left(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\gamma}+\kappa\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{2-2s}\right)+C_{\delta}-M\chi_{R}(\xi)\right)\left|h\right|^{2}\,{\rm d}\xi,

and by taking
M,R>0
sufficiently large, the statement is immediate.
∎

We can now prove the uniform invertibility lemma on
L^{2}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{k})
.

Proof of Lemma
2.10
.

We first prove uniform invertibility estimates
\underline{L}_{\kappa}
, and extend to the uncentred operators by a scaling argument. The operator
\underline{L}_{\kappa}
is symmetric and negative definite on
L^{2}(\underline{M}^{-1/2})
away from its kernel, and therefore admits a self-adjoint Friedrichs extension (cf. Theorem 10.17 of
[
62
]
) and a bounded semigroup
e^{t\underline{L}_{\kappa}}
on
L^{2}(\underline{M}^{-1/2})
. Since
\mathcal{A}:L^{2}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{k})\to L^{2}(\underline{M}^{-1/2})
is a bounded operator for any
k
, using the coercivity estimate in Lemma
A.5
, we can apply Theorem 2.13 of
[
34
]
, and we derive the uniform bound

\displaystyle\left\|\underline{L}_{\kappa}^{-1}f\right\|_{L^{2}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{k})}\leq C_{k}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{k})}

for any microscopic
f\in L^{2}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{k})
.

To prove invertibility of
L_{\mathfrak{u},\kappa}
for
\left|\mathfrak{u}-{\mathfrak{u}_{-}}\right|\leq\epsilon_{0}
, we express
(\rho,u,T)=\mathsf{f}_{0}^{-1}\circ I[\mathfrak{u}]
in hydrodynamic variables, and change the velocity coordinates
\mathsf{V}=T^{-1/2}(\xi-ue_{1})
, but we see that this induces an invertible continuous operator on
L^{2}(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{k})
, and by the scaling identity (
59
), this proves the claim.
∎

Finally, we prove the following technical lemma which we have used in the proof of Lemma
A.2
, which also provides an elementary connection between the non-cutoff Boltzmann operator and fractional Sobolev spaces.

Lemma A.6
.

For
g\in L^{\infty}
, we have the bound

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\!b_{s}(\cos\theta)1_{\left|\xi-\xi_{*}\right|\leq R}g_{*}(h^{\prime}-h)^{2}\,{\rm d}\sigma\,{\rm d}\xi_{*}\,{\rm d}\xi\leq C\left\|h\right\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

where
C
depends on
\left\|g\right\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
R>0
.

This was proved using Littlewood-Paley theory in the proof of Theorem 3.1 of
[
35
]
. We provide here an elementary proof.

Proof.

We define
k=(\xi-\xi_{*})/\left|\xi-\xi_{*}\right|
and
r=\left|\xi-\xi_{*}\right|
, which gives

\displaystyle\xi^{\prime}=\frac{r}{2}(\sigma-k).

By replacing
g
with
\left\|g\right\|_{L^{\infty}}
, we see that we can take
g=1
.
We can then rewrite the integral as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\!\int_{0}^{R}\!b_{s}(\sigma\cdot k)\left[h\left(\xi-\frac{r}{2}(\sigma-k)\right)-h(\xi)\right]^{2}r^{2}\,{\rm d}r\,{\rm d}k\,{\rm d}\sigma\,{\rm d}\xi.

We have the identity

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\!\phi\left(\left|\frac{1}{2}(\sigma-k)\right|\right)\,{\rm d}\sigma\,{\rm d}k

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\!\phi\left(\sqrt{\frac{1-\sigma\cdot k}{2}}\right)\,{\rm d}\sigma\,{\rm d}k

\displaystyle=8\pi^{2}\int_{0}^{\pi}\phi\left(\sqrt{\frac{1-\cos\theta}{2}}\right)\sin\theta\,{\rm d}\theta

\displaystyle=32\pi^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\!\phi(z)z\,{\rm d}z

for
z=\sin(\theta/2)
, so we can rewrite the previous integral as

\displaystyle 32\pi^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\int_{0}^{R}\!b_{s}(1-2z^{2})[h(\xi-rz\sigma)-h(\xi)]r^{2}\,{\rm d}r\,{\rm d}z\,{\rm d}\sigma\,{\rm d}\xi

but using the change of variables
y=rz
, we rewrite this as

\displaystyle 32\pi^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\!\iint_{0\leq R^{-1}y\leq z\leq 1}\!b_{s}(1-2z^{2})[h(\xi-y\sigma)-h(\xi)]yz^{-1}\,{\rm d}y\,{\rm d}z\,{\rm d}\sigma\,{\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\!\iint_{0\leq R^{-1}y\leq z\leq 1}\!z^{-3-2s}[h(\xi-y\sigma)-h(\xi)]y\,{\rm d}y\,{\rm d}z\,{\rm d}\sigma\,{\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad=C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\!\int_{0}^{R}\!(R^{2+2s}y^{-2-2s}-1)[h(\xi-y\sigma)-h(\xi)]y\,{\rm d}y\,{\rm d}\sigma\,{\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{R}^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\!1_{\left|\xi-\xi^{\prime}\right|\leq R}\frac{(h(\xi^{\prime})-h(\xi))^{2}}{\left|\xi-\xi^{\prime}\right|^{3+2s}}\,{\rm d}\xi^{\prime}\,{\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\left\|h\right\|_{H^{s}}^{2}

where we have used the definition of the fractional Sobolev space in
[
22
]
.
∎
